### Mode: reaction-to-carbon-bond-formed (27 questions)

1. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Acid Halide + Amine
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Nitrogen
     2. Carbon-Carbon
     3. Carbon-Sulfur
     4. Carbon-Halogen
     5. Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Nitrogen
   - **Feedback:** Forms an Amide, which has a new C-N at the carbonyl carbon.

2. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Alcohol + Aldehyde
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Oxygen
     3. Carbon-Hydrogen
     4. Carbon-Nitrogen
     5. Carbon-Halogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** Will form a new C-O between the alcohol oxygen and the carbonyl carbon. Addition of 1 eq of alcohol leads to a hemiacetal, 2 eq of alcohol leads to an acetal.

3. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Alcohol + Ketone
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Hydrogen
     3. Carbon-Halogen
     4. Carbon-Oxygen
     5. Carbon-Carbon
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** Will form a new C-O between the alcohol oxygen and the carbonyl carbon. Addition of 1 eq of alcohol leads to a hemiketal, 2 eq of alcohol leads to an ketal.

4. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Aldehyde + Ag+
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Halogen
     2. Carbon-Hydrogen
     3. Carbon-Nitrogen
     4. Carbon-Sulfur
     5. Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** Tollen's Test, where cationic silver is added to a sugar to see if the sugar will reduce Ag+ into Ag(s), which will oxidize the aldehyde into a carboxylic acid.

5. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Aldehyde + LiAlH4
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Oxygen
     3. Carbon-Hydrogen
     4. Carbon-Carbon
     5. Carbon-Nitrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Feedback:** Reduction of an aldehyde into a primary alcohol, forming a new C-H bond.

6. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Aldehyde + NaBH4
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Oxygen
     2. Carbon-Nitrogen
     3. Carbon-Carbon
     4. Carbon-Hydrogen
     5. Carbon-Sulfur
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Feedback:** Reduction of an aldehyde into a primary alcohol, forming a new C-H bond.

7. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Amino Acid + Amino Acid
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Nitrogen
     2. Carbon-Halogen
     3. Carbon-Sulfur
     4. Carbon-Carbon
     5. Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Nitrogen
   - **Feedback:** Forms a new C-N bond between amino acids (an amide bond called the peptide bond).

8. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Carbonyl + R-MgBr
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Nitrogen
     2. Carbon-Halogen
     3. Carbon-Oxygen
     4. Carbon-Hydrogen
     5. Carbon-Carbon
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Carbon
   - **Feedback:** MgBr is the Grignard Reagent that will form a new C-C bond with carbonyl carbons.

9. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Carboxylic Acid + Acid Halide
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Halogen
     2. Carbon-Sulfur
     3. Carbon-Carbon
     4. Carbon-Hydrogen
     5. Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** Halogen leaves as a leaving group, new bond is formed between carboxylic acid oxygen and the acid halide carbon (a new C-O bond)

10. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Carboxylic Acid + Amine + DCC
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Oxygen
     2. Carbon-Carbon
     3. Carbon-Nitrogen
     4. Carbon-Sulfur
     5. Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Nitrogen
   - **Feedback:** Forms an Amide, which has a new C-N at the carbonyl carbon.

11. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Carboxylic Acid + Br2 + PBr3
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Halogen
     2. Carbon-Sulfur
     3. Carbon-Hydrogen
     4. Carbon-Oxygen
     5. Carbon-Nitrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Halogen
   - **Feedback:** Forms a new C-Br bond at the alpha-carbon of the carboxylic acid.

12. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Carboxylic Acid + LiAlH4
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Oxygen
     2. Carbon-Sulfur
     3. Carbon-Halogen
     4. Carbon-Hydrogen
     5. Carbon-Nitrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Feedback:** Reduction of a carboxylic acid into a primary alcohol, forming a new C-H bond.

13. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Carboxylic Acid + SoCl2 or PBr3
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Hydrogen
     2. Carbon-Halogen
     3. Carbon-Carbon
     4. Carbon-Nitrogen
     5. Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Halogen
   - **Feedback:** Forms a new Carbon-Halogen bond between the carbonyl carbon and the halogen (an acid halide is formed).

14. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Carboxylic Acid + Thiol
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Hydrogen
     3. Carbon-Oxygen
     4. Carbon-Nitrogen
     5. Carbon-Halogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Sulfur
   - **Feedback:** Forms a new C-S bond between the carbonyl carbon and the thiol sulfur, making a Thioester.

15. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Carboxylic Acid/Acid Halide + Alcohol
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Nitrogen
     2. Carbon-Sulfur
     3. Carbon-Hydrogen
     4. Carbon-Oxygen
     5. Carbon-Carbon
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** Esterification, where the alcohol oxygen will form a new bond with the carbonyl carbon (a new C-O bond) forming an ester.

16. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Fatty Acid + Glycerol
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Oxygen
     2. Carbon-Nitrogen
     3. Carbon-Sulfur
     4. Carbon-Carbon
     5. Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** New C-O ester bond forms when Tryglycerides are formed (Fatty Acid + Glycerol).

17. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Ketone + LiAlH4
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Carbon
     2. Carbon-Sulfur
     3. Carbon-Halogen
     4. Carbon-Hydrogen
     5. Carbon-Nitrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Feedback:** Reduction of a ketone into a secondary alcohol, forming a new C-H bond.

18. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Ketone + NaBH4
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Halogen
     3. Carbon-Hydrogen
     4. Carbon-Oxygen
     5. Carbon-Carbon
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Feedback:** Reduction of a ketone into a primary alcohol, forming a new C-H bond.

19. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Ketone/Aldehyde + CN
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Hydrogen
     3. Carbon-Halogen
     4. Carbon-Carbon
     5. Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Carbon
   - **Feedback:** Ketone/Aldehyde + HCN/NaCN forms a Cyanohydrin, new C-C bond at Carbonyl Carbon

20. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Ketone/Aldehyde + Ketone/Aldehyde
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Hydrogen
     2. Carbon-Nitrogen
     3. Carbon-Carbon
     4. Carbon-Halogen
     5. Carbon-Sulfur
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Carbon
   - **Feedback:** This would be an Aldol reaction, which forms a new bond between the alpha carbon of one carbonyl with the carbonyl carbon of the other.

21. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Ketone/Aldehyde + Primary Amine
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Nitrogen
     2. Carbon-Sulfur
     3. Carbon-Oxygen
     4. Carbon-Carbon
     5. Carbon-Halogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Nitrogen
   - **Feedback:** Forms an Imine, which is a new C-N bond (specifically C=N bond).

22. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Ketone/Aldehyde + Secondary Amine
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Halogen
     3. Carbon-Carbon
     4. Carbon-Nitrogen
     5. Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Nitrogen
   - **Feedback:** Forms an enamine, which has a new C-N bond.

23. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Monosaccharide + Monosaccharide
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Oxygen
     3. Carbon-Nitrogen
     4. Carbon-Carbon
     5. Carbon-Halogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** Glycosidic Bond Formation, where two sugars are linked by forming a new C-O bond (specifically in a C-O-C ether bond).

24. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Primary Alcohol + Chromium/Manganese Oxides
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Oxygen
     2. Carbon-Nitrogen
     3. Carbon-Sulfur
     4. Carbon-Halogen
     5. Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** Cr/Mn Oxides are very strong oxidizing agents that will fully oxidize (add C-O bonds) carbons where possible, turning primary alcohols into carboxylic acids and secondary into ketones.

25. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Primary Alcohol + PCC
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Nitrogen
     3. Carbon-Oxygen
     4. Carbon-Hydrogen
     5. Carbon-Halogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** PCC Oxidizes Primary Alcohols into Aldehydes (adding in a new C-O bond)

26. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Secondary Alcohol + Chromium/Manganese Oxides
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Nitrogen
     2. Carbon-Hydrogen
     3. Carbon-Oxygen
     4. Carbon-Sulfur
     5. Carbon-Halogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** Cr/Mn Oxides are very strong oxidizing agents that will fully oxidize (add C-O bonds) carbons where possible, turning primary alcohols into carboxylic acids and secondary into ketones.

27. **Question:** What new bond to the carbon chain is formed in the following reaction?: Secondary Alcohol + PCC
   - **Choices:**
     1. Carbon-Sulfur
     2. Carbon-Oxygen
     3. Carbon-Halogen
     4. Carbon-Carbon
     5. Carbon-Hydrogen
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbon-Oxygen
   - **Feedback:** PCC Oxidizes Secondary Alcohols into Ketones (adding in a new C-O bond)

## Category: units

### Mode: ceq (44 questions)

1. **Question:** How can you solve for the rate constant of a reaction?
   - **Choices:**
     1. k[A][B]
     2. [A]e^(-kt)
     3. Ae^(-Ea/RT)
     4. Vmax/[E]
     5. [Products] / [Reactants]
   - **Correct Answer:** Ae^(-Ea/RT)
   - **Feedback:** k = rate constant (lowercase k), which = Ae^(-Ea/RT)  The Arrhenius equation!

2. **Question:** How do you solve for concentration?
   - **Choices:**
     1. mass / volume
     2. mass / kg solvent
     3. moles / L solution
     4. MM / # Equivalents
     5. mol / kg solvent
   - **Correct Answer:** moles / L solution
   - **Feedback:** Molar Concentration (M) = mol / L

3. **Question:** How do you solve for the change in temperature of a freeing point or melting point?
   - **Choices:**
     1. iK(molality)
     2. iK(mass)
     3. mol / L
     4. mCΔT
     5. mL
   - **Correct Answer:** iK(molality)
   - **Feedback:** ΔT = iK(molality), where i is the van't hoff factor, K is a constant that'll be given to you, and m is the molality  For freezing point, a positive number means freezing point decreased. For boiling point, a positive number means freezing point increased.

4. **Question:** How to calculate the heat absorbed or released in a phase change?
   - **Choices:**
     1. iKm
     2. mCΔT
     3. mL
     4. q/ΔT
     5. 𝛼LΔT
   - **Correct Answer:** mL
   - **Feedback:** For phase transitions (where temperature stays constant!), q = mL, where m is the mass of substance and L is a constant for the phase transition given.

5. **Question:** How to convert from C to K?
   - **Choices:**
     1. K = C + 273
     2. C = K + 273
     3. C = K*1.9 + 32
     4. K = C*1.9 + 32
   - **Correct Answer:** K = C + 273
   - **Feedback:** Kelvin is the standard unit of temperature for all subjects, convert all C to K!

6. **Question:** How to solve for an equilibrium constant?
   - **Choices:**
     1. [Products] / [Reactants]
     2. k(forward) / k(backwards)
     3. -nFE
     4. nF/t
     5. 10^-pKeq
   - **Correct Answer:** k(forward) / k(backwards), [Products] / [Reactants]
   - **Feedback:** At equilibrium, Keq = [Products] / [Reactants] and Keq = k(forward) / k(backwards), where k is the rate constant for the forwards or backwards reaction.  Incorrect options: G = -nFE, Current = nF/t, and the other one I made up.

7. **Question:** How to solve for heat capacity of a reaction using a calorimeter?
   - **Choices:**
     1. mL
     2. q/ΔT
     3. mCΔT
     4. iKm
     5. ne
   - **Correct Answer:** q/ΔT
   - **Feedback:** Heat Capacity, C = q/ΔT, where q is the heat of the calorimeter and ΔT is how much the temperature changed.

8. **Question:** How to solve for ppm?
   - **Choices:**
     1. (amount of solute/amount of solvent)*10^6
     2. (amount of solute/amount of solvent)*10^9
     3. (amount of solute/amount of solvent)*10^-6
     4. (amount of solute/amount of solvent)*10^-9
   - **Correct Answer:** (amount of solute/amount of solvent)*10^6
   - **Feedback:** Just make sure that the amount of solute and solvent are in the same unit (grams, liters, moles, particles, etc.)

9. **Question:** How to solve for the energy of a certain electron shell?
   - **Choices:**
     1. ne
     2. -nFE
     3. A * wavephase
     4. hf
     5. -13.6eV / n^2
   - **Correct Answer:** -13.6eV / n^2
   - **Feedback:** E (of energy shell, n) = -13.6eV / n^2

10. **Question:** How to solve for the heat absorbed or released in a reaction?
   - **Choices:**
     1. mCΔT
     2. mL
     3. q/ΔT
     4. iKm
     5. 𝛂(obs) / cL
   - **Correct Answer:** mCΔT
   - **Feedback:** q = mCΔT, where q is the amount of heat released or absorbed in the reaction (measured in J)

11. **Question:** How to solve for the number of different orders that a certain number (n) of alleles or amino acids can have?
   - **Choices:**
     1. ne
     2. 2n^2
     3. 2^n
     4. n!
   - **Correct Answer:** n!
   - **Feedback:** If you're trying to figure out how many different ways you can ORDER an n number of alleles or amino acids, it's n! (n factorial)  If you're just trying to figure out how many different combinations of alleles or amino acids there are, it's 2^n

12. **Question:** How to solve for the specific rotation of a molecule?
   - **Choices:**
     1. 𝛂(obs) / cL
     2. mL
     3. mCΔT
     4. A * wavephase
     5. v^2 / r
   - **Correct Answer:** 𝛂(obs) / cL
   - **Feedback:** [𝛂] = 𝛂(obs) / cL, where [𝛂] is the specific rotation in degrees, 𝛂(obs) is the observed rotation, c is the concentration (often in g/mL), and L is path length (often in dm)  They will probably specify the units for you.

13. **Question:** How to solve for thermal expansion of a substance?
   - **Choices:**
     1. q/ΔT
     2. mL
     3. Q/n
     4. 𝛼LΔT
     5. ΔH - TΔS
   - **Correct Answer:** 𝛼LΔT
   - **Feedback:** ΔL = 𝛼LΔT, where ΔL is how much the length changed, L is the original length, 𝛼 is a constant that'll be given to you, and ΔT is how much the temp changed (in K).

14. **Question:** Select all valid ways to increase the rate constant for a given reaction
   - **Choices:**
     1. Increasing Temperature
     2. Decreasing Pressure
     3. Increasing the Probability of Collision
     4. Decreasing Activation Energy
     5. Increasing Activation Energy
   - **Correct Answer:** Decreasing Activation Energy, Increasing the Probability of Collision, Increasing Temperature
   - **Feedback:** k = Ae^(-Ea/RT), So increasing A (probability of collision) and T will increase k, decreasing Ea will increase k

15. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for [H+]
   - **Choices:**
     1. 10^-pH
     2. pOH + pH
     3. Kw/Ka
     4. -PΔV
     5. (3/2)nRT
   - **Correct Answer:** 10^-pH
   - **Feedback:** [H] = 10^-pH

16. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for current
   - **Choices:**
     1. V/R
     2. Q/t
     3. nF/t
     4. CV
     5. n/V
   - **Correct Answer:** nF/t, Q/t, V/R
   - **Feedback:** Incorrect answer choices: Q = CV and Molar Concentration  = moles / volume

17. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for entropy of a reaction
   - **Choices:**
     1. ⅀ΔS(products) - ⅀ΔS(reactants)
     2. ⅀ΔS(reactants) - ⅀ΔS(products)
     3. Q/n
     4. Q/T
     5. mCΔT
   - **Correct Answer:** Q/T, ⅀ΔS(products) - ⅀ΔS(reactants)
   - **Feedback:** Entropy = ΔS = ⅀ΔS(products) - ⅀ΔS(reactants) or Q/T, where Q is the heat of a reversible reaction, and T is temperature  Note the units of entropy are J/K!

18. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for Gibbs Free Energy
   - **Choices:**
     1. -nFE
     2. -RTlnKeq
     3. ΔH - TΔS
     4. (ΣΔG of Products) - (ΣΔG of Reactants)
     5. Q/T
   - **Correct Answer:** -nFE, (ΣΔG of Products) - (ΣΔG of Reactants), ΔH - TΔS, -RTlnKeq
   - **Feedback:** Q/T is a way of solving for Entropy (S), not ΔG

19. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for Gram Equivalents
   - **Choices:**
     1. Molar Mass / # Equivalents
     2. moles / kg solvent
     3. moles / L solvent
     4. -nFE
     5. X*Ptotal
   - **Correct Answer:** Molar Mass / # Equivalents
   - **Feedback:** Gram Equivalents = Molar Mass / # Equivalents  Incorrect options: moles / kg solvent = molality, moles / L solvent = concentration, G = -nFE, Partial Pressure = X*Ptotal

20. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for Ka
   - **Choices:**
     1. Kw/Kb
     2. 10^-pKa
     3. 10^-pKb
     4. 10^-pH
     5. [CB][H+] / [Acid]
   - **Correct Answer:** [CB][H+] / [Acid], Kw/Kb, 10^-pKa
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

21. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for Kb
   - **Choices:**
     1. Kw/Ka
     2. Ka*Kb
     3. pOH + pH
     4. 10^-pKb
     5. [CA][OH-] / [Base]
   - **Correct Answer:** Kw/Ka, 10^-pKb, [CA][OH-] / [Base]
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

22. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for Normality
   - **Choices:**
     1. (Molar Mass of the Substance) / (Number of Equivalents)
     2. [Acid or Base] * Number of equivalents
     3. pH + pOH
     4. Vmax/[E]
     5. mol / L solvent
   - **Correct Answer:** [Acid or Base] * Number of equivalents
   - **Feedback:** Used whenever you have polyprotic acids or polyvalent bases instead of concentration, since 1 mol of a polyprotic acid (for example) will produce multiple moles of H+  Incorrect options: MM / # eq = Gram Equivalents, pH + pOH = 14, kcat = Vmax / [E], and mol / L = concentration

23. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for osmotic pressure
   - **Choices:**
     1. mol / kg solvent
     2. mol / L
     3. F / A
     4. iMRT
     5. iKm
   - **Correct Answer:** iMRT
   - **Feedback:** Pi Mirt! Ⲡ = iMRT, where Ⲡ is osmotic pressure, i is the van't hoff factor, M is the concentration of solution, R is the gas constant, and T is the temperature (in K)

24. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for pH
   - **Choices:**
     1. [H] = 10^-pH
     2. 14 - pOH
     3. Ka*Kb
     4. M1V1 = M2V2
     5. pH = -log[OH]
   - **Correct Answer:** 14 - pOH, [H] = 10^-pH
   - **Feedback:** [H] = 10^-pH and pH + pOH = 14 are both valid ways to solve for pH

25. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for pOH
   - **Choices:**
     1. 14 - pH
     2. 10^-pKa
     3. [OH] = 10^-pOH
     4. Ka*Kb
     5. (pKa1 + pKa2)/2
   - **Correct Answer:** [OH] = 10^-pOH, 14 - pH
   - **Feedback:** pH + pOH = 14, [OH] = 10^-pOH

26. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for pressure
   - **Choices:**
     1. F/A
     2. W/ΔV
     3. nRT/V
     4. Q/T
     5. (3/2)nRT
   - **Correct Answer:** nRT/V, W/ΔV, F/A
   - **Feedback:** Correct Equations: P = F/A, PV = nRT, and W = -PΔV  Incorrect Options: ΔS = Q/T and Gas Internal Energy = (3/2)nRT

27. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for the enthalpy of a reaction
   - **Choices:**
     1. (⅀ΔH(products)) - (⅀ΔH(reactants))
     2. (⅀ΔH(reactants)) - (⅀ΔH(products))
     3. (⅀ΔH(bonds broken)) - (⅀ΔH(bonds formed))
     4. (⅀ΔH(bonds formed)) - (⅀ΔH(bonds broken))
     5. Q/n
   - **Correct Answer:** (⅀ΔH(products)) - (⅀ΔH(reactants)), (⅀ΔH(bonds broken)) - (⅀ΔH(bonds formed)), Q/n
   - **Feedback:** Ways to solve for ΔH: ΔH = (⅀ΔH(bonds broken)) - (⅀ΔH(bonds formed)) ΔH = (⅀ΔH(products)) - (⅀ΔH(reactants)) ΔH = Q/n, where Q is the heat (in J) and n is number of moles

28. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for the Internal Energy of a Gas
   - **Choices:**
     1. (3/2)nRT
     2. (1/2)mv^2
     3. (3/2)kT
     4. -PΔV
     5. -nFE
   - **Correct Answer:** (3/2)nRT
   - **Feedback:** Internal Energy = Potential Energy (U), for a Gas, U = (3/2)nRT  Incorrect Options: KE = (1/2)mv^2 = (3/2)kT, W = -PΔV, and ΔG = -nFE

29. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for the ionization constant of water
   - **Choices:**
     1. Ka*Kb
     2. pKa + pKb
     3. 10^-pKa
     4. 10^-pKa + 10^-pKb
     5. (pKa1 + pKa2)/2
   - **Correct Answer:** Ka*Kb
   - **Feedback:** Ionization constant of water is Kw, which = Kq*Kb

30. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for the kinetic energy of a gas
   - **Choices:**
     1. (3/2)kT
     2. (1/2)mv^2
     3. (3/2)nRT
     4. mgh
     5. nRT/P
   - **Correct Answer:** (3/2)kT, (1/2)mv^2
   - **Feedback:** KE (gas) = (1/2)mv^2 = (3/2)kT  Incorrect options: U(gas) = (3/2)nRT, Force of Gravity = mgh, and PV = nRT

31. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for the rate of effusion of a gas
   - **Choices:**
     1. rate1/rate2 = sqrt(mass2/mass1)
     2. rate2/rate1 = sqrt(mass2/mass1)
     3. (1/2)mv^2
     4. PV = nRT
     5. (3/2)kT
   - **Correct Answer:** rate1/rate2 = sqrt(mass2/mass1)
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

32. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for the Work done by gasses
   - **Choices:**
     1. -PΔV
     2. F/A
     3. (3/2)nRT
     4. W/t
     5. nF/t
   - **Correct Answer:** -PΔV
   - **Feedback:** W = -PΔV  Incorrect options: Pressure = F/A, U(gas) = (3/2)nRT, Power = W/t, Current = nF/t

33. **Question:** What equation(s) describe the allele frequency in Hardy-Weinberg equilibrium?
   - **Choices:**
     1. p + q = 1
     2. p^2 + 2pq + q^2 = 1
     3. 2pq
     4. p^2
     5. q^2
   - **Correct Answer:** p + q = 1
   - **Feedback:** p + q = 1 says that the frequency of the dominant (p) allele + the frequency of the recessive (q) allele must = 1

34. **Question:** What is the average mass of 1 amino acid?
   - **Choices:**
     1. 110 kDa
     2. 110 Da
     3. 1.1 kDa
     4. 0.11 Da
     5. 3.6 Da
   - **Correct Answer:** 110 Da
   - **Feedback:** 1 AA = 110 Da = 0.11 kDa  Note you can round this to 100 Da (or 0.1 kDa) 99% of the time on the exam.

35. **Question:** What is the equation for catalytic turnover?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Vmax/[E]
     2. kcat/Km
     3. 1/Vmax
     4. (Vmax*[S]/(Km+[S]))
     5. Km/Vmax
   - **Correct Answer:** Vmax/[E]
   - **Feedback:** Catalytic turnover = kcat, which = Vmax/[E]

36. **Question:** What is the equation for the efficiency of an enzyme?
   - **Choices:**
     1. -1/Vmax
     2. Vmax/[E]
     3. kcat/Km
     4. -1/Km
     5. Ae^(-Ea/RT)
   - **Correct Answer:** kcat/Km
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

37. **Question:** What is the equation for the number of combinations a certain number of alleles or amino acids (n) can combine in
   - **Choices:**
     1. 2n^2
     2. 2^n
     3. n!
     4. p + q = 1
   - **Correct Answer:** 2^n
   - **Feedback:** The number of different allele or amino acid sequence combinations for an n number of amino acids or alleles is 2^n.  The number of different orders those amino acids or alleles can occupy would be n! (n factorial)

38. **Question:** What is the Michaelis Menten Equation?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Vo = (Vmax*[S]/(Km+[S]))
     2. Vmax = (Vo*[S]/(Km+[S])
     3. Vo = (Vmax*Km/[S])
     4. Vmax = (Vo*[S]/(Km+Vo)
     5. Vo = (Vmax*Km/(Km+[S]))
   - **Correct Answer:** Vo = (Vmax*[S]/(Km+[S]))
   - **Feedback:** Vo = (Vmax*[S]/(Km+[S])) is the Michaelis Menten equation. Produces a hyperbolic curve that initially starts as a 1st order reaction and turns into a 0th order reaction as Vmax is reached.

39. **Question:** What is the proper way to solve for the number of turns in a given alpha helices?
   - **Choices:**
     1. # AAs * 4.1
     2. # AAs / 110 Da
     3. # AAs * 110 Da
     4. # AAs / 3.6
     5. # AAs * pKa
   - **Correct Answer:** # AAs / 3.6
   - **Feedback:** There are 3.6 amino acids per turn, so the total number of turns is Total AAs / 3.6

40. **Question:** What is the slope equal to in a Lineweaver-Burk Plot?
   - **Choices:**
     1. kcat/Km
     2. Vmax/[E]
     3. 1/Vmax
     4. -1/Km
     5. Km/Vmax
   - **Correct Answer:** Km/Vmax
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

41. **Question:** What is the x-int equal to in Lineweaver-Burk Plots?
   - **Choices:**
     1. -1/Km
     2. 1/Km
     3. 1/Vmax
     4. -1/Vmax
     5. Km/Vmax
   - **Correct Answer:** -1/Km
   - **Feedback:** Increasing the Km will move the x-int closer to 0

42. **Question:** What is the y-intercept in a Lineweaver-Burk Plot?
   - **Choices:**
     1. -1/Km
     2. 1/Km
     3. -1/Vmax
     4. Km/Vmax
     5. 1/Vmax
   - **Correct Answer:** 1/Vmax
   - **Feedback:** Increasing Vmax decreases the y-int

43. **Question:** Which equation demonstrates the frequency of different genotypes (full equation or individual genotypes) in Hardy-Weinberg equilibrium?
   - **Choices:**
     1. 2pq
     2. p^2
     3. q^2
     4. p^2 + 2pq + q^2 = 1
     5. p + q = 1
   - **Correct Answer:** p^2 + 2pq + q^2 = 1, q^2, p^2, 2pq
   - **Feedback:** In the equation p^2 + 2pq + q^2 = 1, p^2 is the probability of an organism being homozygous dominant, q^2 is the probability of an organism being recessive, and 2pq is the probability of an organism being heterozygous

44. **Question:** Which of the following is equal to 1 atm?
   - **Choices:**
     1. 760 Torr
     2. 760 mmHg
     3. 100 Pa
     4. 100 kPa
     5. 100 Torr
   - **Correct Answer:** 100 kPa, 760 mmHg, 760 Torr
   - **Feedback:** Pressure: 1 atm = 760 torr or mmHg = 100 kPa (rounded!)

### Mode: default (51 questions)

1. **Question:** a
   - **Choices:**
     1. Meters per Second Squared (m/s²)
     2. Farads (F)
     3. Grams (g)
     4. Ohms (omega)
   - **Correct Answer:** Meters per Second Squared (m/s²)
   - **Feedback:** a = acceleration

2. **Question:** Amps (A)
   - **Choices:** _(none provided)_
   - **Correct Answer:** I (current)
   - **Feedback:** I = Current

3. **Question:** Atmospheres (atm)
   - **Choices:** _(none provided)_
   - **Correct Answer:** P (pressure)
   - **Feedback:** P = pressure

4. **Question:** C
   - **Choices:**

### Mode: lenses_and_mirrors (23 questions)

1. **Question:** At what object distance for converging systems are images at their largest size?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Approaching the focal point
     2. Near the lens
     3. Far from the lens
   - **Correct Answer:** Approaching the focal point
   - **Feedback:** Objects in converging systems get larger as they approach the focal length.

2. **Question:** At what object distance for diverging systems are images at their largest size?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Near the lens
     2. Far from the lens
     3. Approaching the focal point
   - **Correct Answer:** Near the lens
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

3. **Question:** How do you solve for Power of a Lens?
   - **Choices:**
     1. 1/f
     2. -i/o
     3. hi/hf
     4. 1/r
   - **Correct Answer:** 1/f
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

4. **Question:** How is Hyperopia treated?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Converging Lens
     2. Diverging Lens
   - **Correct Answer:** Converging Lens
   - **Feedback:** Power of the eye isn't strong enough when objects are close, so a converging lens is added to increase the power.

5. **Question:** How is Myopia treated?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Converging Lens
     2. Diverging Lens
   - **Correct Answer:** Diverging Lens
   - **Feedback:** Power of the lens of the eye is too strong for objects that are far away, so a Diverging Lens is added to decrease the power of the eye.

6. **Question:** If there are multiple lenses in a row, how do we calculate the total Magnification of the lenses?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Add them
     2. Divide them
     3. Subtract them
     4. Multiply them
   - **Correct Answer:** Multiply them
   - **Feedback:** Find the magnification of each lens and multiply them all together to get total magnification

7. **Question:** If there are multiple lenses in a row, how do we calculate the total Power of the lenses?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Add them
     2. Subtract them
     3. Multiply them
     4. Divide them
   - **Correct Answer:** Add them
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

8. **Question:** Select all lenses and mirrors that are Converging Systems
   - **Choices:**
     1. Convex Lens
     2. Concave Lens
     3. Convex Mirror
     4. Concave Mirror
   - **Correct Answer:** Convex Lens, Concave Mirror
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

9. **Question:** Select all lenses and mirrors that are Diverging Systems
   - **Choices:**
     1. Convex Mirror
     2. Concave Mirror
     3. Convex Lens
     4. Concave Lens
   - **Correct Answer:** Convex Mirror, Concave Lens
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

10. **Question:** Select all lenses and mirrors that can create real, inverted images
   - **Choices:**
     1. Convex Lens
     2. Convex Mirror
     3. Concave Lens
     4. Concave Mirror
   - **Correct Answer:** Convex Lens, Concave Mirror
   - **Feedback:** Only converging systems can create real, inverted images.

11. **Question:** Select all lenses and mirrors that can create virtual, upright images
   - **Choices:**
     1. Convex Mirror
     2. Concave Mirror
     3. Convex Lens
     4. Concave Lens
   - **Correct Answer:** Convex Mirror, Concave Mirror, Convex Lens, Concave Lens
   - **Feedback:** All systems can create virtual, upright images

12. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for Magnification of a Lens?
   - **Choices:**
     1. -i/o
     2. hi/ho
     3. 1/f
     4. o/i
   - **Correct Answer:** -i/o, hi/ho
   - **Feedback:** M = -i/o = image height / object height

13. **Question:** What are the possible images that converging systems can make?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Virtual Upright
     2. Real Inverted
     3. Virtual Inverted
     4. Real Upright
   - **Correct Answer:** Virtual Upright, Real Inverted
   - **Feedback:** Virtual Inverted and Real Upright images don't exist

14. **Question:** What are the possible images that diverging systems can make?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Virtual Upright
     2. Virtual Inverted
     3. Real Upright
     4. Real Inverted
   - **Correct Answer:** Virtual Upright
   - **Feedback:** Virtual Inverted and Real Upright images don't exist

15. **Question:** What classification is a concave lens?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Converging System
     2. Diverging System
   - **Correct Answer:** Diverging System
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

16. **Question:** What classification is a concave mirror?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Diverging System
     2. Converging System
   - **Correct Answer:** Converging System
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

17. **Question:** What classification is a convex lens?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Converging System
     2. Diverging System
   - **Correct Answer:** Converging System
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

18. **Question:** What classification is a convex mirror?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Diverging System
     2. Converging System
   - **Correct Answer:** Diverging System
   - **Feedback:** Diverging systems (convex mirrors and concave lenses) are those that can only make virtual, upright images.

19. **Question:** What is the lens equation?
   - **Choices:**
     1. 1/o + 1/i = 1/f
     2. 1/f + 1/o = 1/i
     3. 1/i + 1/f = 1/o
     4. -i/o
   - **Correct Answer:** 1/o + 1/i = 1/f
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

20. **Question:** What is the relationship between Focal Length and Power?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Inverse
     2. Proportional
   - **Correct Answer:** Inverse
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

21. **Question:** What is the relationship between Focal Length and Radius of Curvature?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Inverse
     2. Proportional
   - **Correct Answer:** Proportional
   - **Feedback:** r = 2f, so as one increases, as does the other

22. **Question:** Which systems create both types of images?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Converging Systems
     2. Diverging Systems
   - **Correct Answer:** Converging Systems
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

23. **Question:** Which systems create only virtual, upright images?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Converging Systems
     2. Diverging Systems
   - **Correct Answer:** Diverging Systems
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

   - **Choices:**
     1. ρgh
     2. (1/2)ρv^2
     3. ρVg
     4. mgh
     5. Pabs - Patm
   - **Correct Answer:** ρgh, Pabs - Patm
   - **Feedback:** Pg = ρgh and Pabs = Patm + Pg

2. **Question:** Select all valid ways to solve for Pressure?
   - **Choices:**
     1. F/A
     2. ρgh
     3. P/A
     4. nRT/V
     5. ρV/L
   - **Correct Answer:** F/A, nRT/V
   - **Feedback:** PV = nRT and P = F/A

3. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for % Obj Submerged
   - **Choices:**
     1. density of the object / density of the fluid
     2. Patm + Pg
     3. pgh
     4. mgh
     5. pVg
   - **Correct Answer:** density of the object / density of the fluid
   - **Feedback:** Ratio of densities will tell you specific gravity, which is what percent of the object will be submerged

4. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Amount of Charge for a certain number of particles
   - **Choices:**
     1. ne
     2. hf
     3. kqq/r^2
     4. Ed
     5. qd
   - **Correct Answer:** ne
   - **Feedback:** Q = ne, where Q is the total charge, n is the number of particles (via Avogadro's number), and e is the fundamental charge of an electron

5. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for beat frequency
   - **Choices:**
     1. abs value(f1-f2)
     2. nv/2L
     3. 2L/n
     4. nv/4L
     5. ma
   - **Correct Answer:** abs value(f1-f2)
   - **Feedback:** The absolute value of the differences between the two frequencies

6. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Buoyant Force
   - **Choices:**
     1. Fnet - Fg
     2. ρVg
     3. ρgh
     4. Patm + Pg
     5. Av
   - **Correct Answer:** Fnet - Fg, ρVg
   - **Feedback:** Fnet = Fg - Fb and Fb = pVg

7. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Cardiac Output
   - **Choices:**
     1. HR*SV
     2. pgh
     3. pVg
     4. v/t
     5. Av
   - **Correct Answer:** HR*SV
   - **Feedback:** CO = Heart Rate * Stroke Volume

8. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Centripetal (Rotational) Acceleration
   - **Choices:**
     1. v^2/r
     2. (1/2)mv^2
     3. mgh
     4. mv
     5. vt/2
   - **Correct Answer:** v^2/r
   - **Feedback:** Centripetal Acceleration = v^2/r

9. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Centripetal Force
   - **Choices:**
     1. m(v^2)/r
     2. (1/2)mv^2
     3. mgh
     4. mv
     5. vt/3
   - **Correct Answer:** m(v^2)/r
   - **Feedback:** Centripetal Force = m(v^2)/r

10. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Conductivity
   - **Choices:**
     1. 1/R
     2. V/I
     3. Q/t
     4. V/R
     5. qd
   - **Correct Answer:** 1/R
   - **Feedback:** Conductivity is the inverse of Resistance

11. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Current
   - **Choices:**
     1. Q/t
     2. V/R
     3. P/V
     4. pL/A
     5. qd
   - **Correct Answer:** Q/t, V/R, P/V
   - **Feedback:** I = Q/t, V = IR, and P = IV

12. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Dielectric Strength
   - **Choices:**
     1. kC
     2. CV
     3. Q/V
     4. IR
     5. Ed
   - **Correct Answer:** kC
   - **Feedback:** C' = kC

13. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Dipole Moment
   - **Choices:**
     1. qd
     2. (1/2)mv^2
     3. V/d
     4. qE
     5. kQ/r^2
   - **Correct Answer:** qd
   - **Feedback:** p = qd

14. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Efficiency?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Po/Pi
     2. m(v^2)/r
     3. ma
     4. Fv
     5. F out / F in
   - **Correct Answer:** Po/Pi
   - **Feedback:** Efficiency = Power Output / Power Input, Efficiency is a measurement of Power, the ratio of Forces is Mechanical Advantage

15. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Elastic Potential Energy
   - **Choices:**
     1. (1/2)kx^2
     2. -kx
     3. kqq/r^2
     4. qvBsin(theta)
     5. qE
   - **Correct Answer:** (1/2)kx^2
   - **Feedback:** U = (1/2)kx^2

16. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Electric Field
   - **Choices:**
     1. kQ/r^2
     2. F/q
     3. V/d
     4. qV
     5. kqq/r
   - **Correct Answer:** kQ/r^2, qV, V/d, F/q
   - **Feedback:** E = kQ/r^2 = qV = V/d, and F = qE (so E = F/q)

17. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Electric Force
   - **Choices:**
     1. qE
     2. V/d
     3. Q/eA
     4. CV
     5. kqq/r^2
   - **Correct Answer:** qE, kqq/r^2
   - **Feedback:** Fe = qE = kqq/r^2

18. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Energy of a Wave
   - **Choices:**
     1. hf
     2. hc/λ
     3. 1/f
     4. λ*f
     5. W/t
   - **Correct Answer:** hf, hc/λ
   - **Feedback:** E = hf = hc/λ

19. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for for Elastic/Spring Force
   - **Choices:**
     1. -kx
     2. (1/2)kx^2
     3. kqq/r^2
     4. qvBsin(theta)
     5. qE
   - **Correct Answer:** -kx
   - **Feedback:** F = -kx

20. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Force between Two Charges
   - **Choices:**
     1. kqq/r^2
     2. kqq/r
     3. iLBsin(theta)
     4. qvBsin(theta)
     5. qE
   - **Correct Answer:** kqq/r^2, qE
   - **Feedback:** Fq = kqq/r^2 = qE

21. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Force of Gravity parallel to an inclined plane?
   - **Choices:**
     1. mgsin(theta)
     2. mgcos(theta)
     3. mg
     4. mg*tan(theta)
     5. mu*Fn
   - **Correct Answer:** mgsin(theta)
   - **Feedback:** Fg on an inclined plane is equal to the parallel component, which is mgsin(theta).

22. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for frequency of a closed pipe on one end.
   - **Choices:**
     1. nv/4L
     2. nv/2L
     3. 2L/n
     4. 4L/n
     5. ma
   - **Correct Answer:** nv/4L
   - **Feedback:** By convention, a "closed pipe" is only closed on one end.

23. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for frequency of an open pipe
   - **Choices:**
     1. nv/2L
     2. nv/4L
     3. 2L/n
     4. 4L/n
     5. ma
   - **Correct Answer:** nv/2L
   - **Feedback:** f = nv/2L, where n = 1, 2, 3...

24. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Friction Force
   - **Choices:**
     1. mu*Fn
     2. mu*Fg
     3. ma
     4. F/d
     5. Fv
   - **Correct Answer:** mu*Fn
   - **Feedback:** F(friction) = mu*Normal Force

25. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Gravitational Force Between Two Objects
   - **Choices:**
     1. G(Mm)/r^2
     2. kqq/r^2
     3. rho*gh
     4. v/t
     5. qd
   - **Correct Answer:** G(Mm)/r^2
   - **Feedback:** Fg = G(Mm)/r^2

26. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Gravitational Potential Energy
   - **Choices:**
     1. mgh
     2. ma
     3. qE
     4. mgcos(theta)
     5. F/d
   - **Correct Answer:** mgh
   - **Feedback:** Ug = mgh

27. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for index of refraction
   - **Choices:**
     1. c/v
     2. n(sin(theta1))/(sin(theta2))
     3. hf
     4. 2L/n
     5. kC
   - **Correct Answer:** c/v, n(sin(theta1))/(sin(theta2))
   - **Feedback:** Snells Law: n1(sin(theta1)) = n2(sin(theta2)), and n = c/v, where c is the speed of light in a vacuum and v is the velocity through the medium

28. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Intensity
   - **Choices:**
     1. P/A
     2. IV
     3. qEd
     4. F/A
     5. W/t
   - **Correct Answer:** P/A
   - **Feedback:** Int = P/A

29. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for KE of an Ejected Particle
   - **Choices:**
     1. (1/2)mv^2
     2. E - Work Function
     3. hv
     4. vt/2
     5. mgh
   - **Correct Answer:** (1/2)mv^2, E - Work Function
   - **Feedback:** KE = (1/2)mv^2, in the Photoelectric Effect: KE = E - Work Function, where E is the energy of the incident wave

30. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Magnetic Field Force
   - **Choices:**
     1. qvBsin(theta)
     2. iLBsin(theta)
     3. -kx
     4. kqq/r^2
     5. qE
   - **Correct Answer:** qvBsin(theta), iLBsin(theta), qvBsin(theta), iLBsin(theta), iLBsin(theta), qvBsin(theta)
   - **Feedback:** Fb = qvBsin(theta) = iLBsin(theta)

31. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Magnification of a Lens
   - **Choices:**
     1. -i/o
     2. hi/ho
     3. 1/f
     4. (1/2)r
     5. c/v
   - **Correct Answer:** -i/o, hi/ho
   - **Feedback:** M = -i/o = hi/ho

32. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Maximum Number of Electrons in a Shell
   - **Choices:**
     1. 2n^2
     2. 2n
     3. N!
     4. hf
     5. ne
   - **Correct Answer:** 2n^2
   - **Feedback:** number of electrons per shell = 2n^2, where n is the energy level

33. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Mechanical Advantage
   - **Choices:**
     1. F(out) / F(in)
     2. density of the object / density of the fluid
     3. mgh
     4. ma
     5. Po/Pi
   - **Correct Answer:** F(out) / F(in)
   - **Feedback:** MA = Force without Assistance (out) / Force with Assistance (in), Power out over Power in (or energy in joules over energy in joules) is for efficiency, not MA

34. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Momentum
   - **Choices:**
     1. mv
     2. ma
     3. F/d
     4. mgh
     5. pVg
   - **Correct Answer:** mv
   - **Feedback:** p = mv

35. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Normal Force on an inclined plane
   - **Choices:**
     1. mgcos(theta)
     2. mgsin(theta)
     3. mg
     4. mg*tan(theta)
     5. mu*Fn
   - **Correct Answer:** mgcos(theta)
   - **Feedback:** Normal Force = mgcos(theta)

36. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Period
   - **Choices:**
     1. 1/f
     2. λ/v
     3. hf
     4. Q/t
     5. hc/λ
   - **Correct Answer:** 1/f, λ/v
   - **Feedback:** T = 1/f = λ/v, since v = λ*f

37. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Potential Energy of a Capacitor
   - **Choices:**
     1. (1/2)CV^2
     2. IV
     3. Ed
     4. Q/t
     5. CV
   - **Correct Answer:** (1/2)CV^2
   - **Feedback:** Uc = (1/2)CV^2

38. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Potential Energy of a Point Charge or between two Point Charges?
   - **Choices:**
     1. kqq/r
     2. qV
     3. kqq/r^2
     4. kQ/r
     5. (1/2)kx^2
   - **Correct Answer:** kqq/r, qV
   - **Feedback:** Uq = kqq/r = qV

39. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Power
   - **Choices:**
     1. IV
     2. ΔE/t
     3. ΔW/t
     4. Fv
     5. qV
   - **Correct Answer:** IV, ΔE/t, ΔW/t, Fv
   - **Feedback:** P = IV = ΔE/t = ΔW/t = Fv

40. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Power of a Lens
   - **Choices:**
     1. 1/f
     2. 2/r
     3. c/v
     4. -i/o
     5. IV
   - **Correct Answer:** 1/f, 2/r
   - **Feedback:** P = 1/f, and f = (1/2)r so P = 2/r

41. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Resistance
   - **Choices:**
     1. V/I
     2. pL/A
     3. 1/Conductivity
     4. IV
     5. Ed
   - **Correct Answer:** V/I, pL/A, 1/Conductivity
   - **Feedback:** From V=IR, and R = pL/A, and R = 1/Conductivity

42. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for the distance of an object from an ultrasound
   - **Choices:**
     1. vt/2
     2. d2-d1
     3. hf
     4. 2n
     5. nv/4L
   - **Correct Answer:** vt/2
   - **Feedback:** distance from the ultrasound to an object = vt/2

43. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Thermal Expansion
   - **Choices:**
     1. alpha*L*ΔT
     2. mCΔT
     3. mL
     4. ma
     5. mgh
   - **Correct Answer:** alpha*L*ΔT
   - **Feedback:** Thermal Expansion = alpha*L*ΔT, alpha is a constant to the material, L is the length, and T is the change in temperature

44. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Torque
   - **Choices:**
     1. rF
     2. mgh
     3. m(v^2)/r
     4. v^2/r
     5. mv
   - **Correct Answer:** rF
   - **Feedback:** Torque = rF, Torques will equal out when objects are in rotational equilibrium

45. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Velocity of a Wave
   - **Choices:**
     1. E*lamda/h
     2. lamda*f
     3. hf
     4. Q/t
     5. v/t
   - **Correct Answer:** E*lamda/h, lamda*f
   - **Feedback:** From v = lambda*f and E = hf = hc/lambda

46. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Voltage
   - **Choices:**
     1. kQ/r
     2. P/I
     3. Ed
     4. IR
     5. Q/t
   - **Correct Answer:** kQ/r, P/I, Ed, IR
   - **Feedback:** V = kQ/r, V = IR, P = IV, and E = V/d

47. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Volumetric Flow Rate
   - **Choices:**
     1. Av
     2. pgh
     3. pVg
     4. v/t
     5. HR*SV
   - **Correct Answer:** Av
   - **Feedback:** Q = Av, Q is constant in ideal fluids

48. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for wavelength of a closed pipe on one end.
   - **Choices:**
     1. 4L/n
     2. nv/2L
     3. 2L/n
     4. nv/4L
     5. ma
   - **Correct Answer:** 4L/n
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

49. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for wavelength of an open pipe
   - **Choices:**
     1. 2L/n
     2. nv/2L
     3. 4L/n
     4. nv/4L
     5. ma
   - **Correct Answer:** 2L/n
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

50. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Wavenumber
   - **Choices:**
     1. 1/λ
     2. hf
     3. hc/λ
     4. λ*f
     5. qd
   - **Correct Answer:** 1/λ
   - **Feedback:** Wavenumber = 1/λ (inverse of wavelength)

51. **Question:** Select valid ways to solve for Work
   - **Choices:**
     1. Fd
     2. qEd
     3. qV
     4. (delta)U
     5. W/t
   - **Correct Answer:** Fd, qEd, qV, (delta)U
   - **Feedback:** W = Fd = qEd = qV = (delta)U (potential energy)

### Mode: physics_equations (1 questions)

1. **Question:** How do you solve for the propagation speed of a wave using it's phase?
   - **Choices:**
     1. A * wave phase
     2. hf
     3. hc/λ
     4. λf
     5. (1/2)mv^2
   - **Correct Answer:** A * wave phase
   - **Feedback:** propagation speed = y(x,t) = A * wave phase, where A is the displacement and the wave phase will contain information about the displacement of time of the wave (and therefore velocity inside of the wave phase)

### Mode: single_choice (24 questions)

1. **Question:** How do you interpret the results of H-NMR?
   - **Choices:**
     1. The more deshielded the hydrogen, the higher the chemical shift of the hydrogen (further left).
     2. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     3. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     4. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     5. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
   - **Correct Answer:** The more deshielded the hydrogen, the higher the chemical shift of the hydrogen (further left).
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

2. **Question:** In IR, what functional group is present if there are two sharp peaks around 3300-3400?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     2. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     3. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     4. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     5. Primary Amine
   - **Correct Answer:** Primary Amine
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

3. **Question:** In IR, what functional group is present if there is a broad peak around 3200-3600?
   - **Choices:**
     1. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     2. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     3. Alcohol
     4. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     5. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
   - **Correct Answer:** Alcohol
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

4. **Question:** In IR, what functional group is present if there is a sharp peak around 1700-1725?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     2. Carbonyl
     3. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     4. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     5. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
   - **Correct Answer:** Carbonyl
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

5. **Question:** In IR, what functional group is present if there is a sharp peak around 2100-2300?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     2. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     3. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     4. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     5. Triple Bond (either C-C or C-N)
   - **Correct Answer:** Triple Bond (either C-C or C-N)
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

6. **Question:** In IR, what functional group is present if there is one sharp peak around 3300-3400?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     2. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     3. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     4. Secondary Amine
     5. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
   - **Correct Answer:** Secondary Amine
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

7. **Question:** In Mass Spec, what does the Base peak tell us?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     2. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     3. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     4. The most abundant fragment
     5. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
   - **Correct Answer:** The most abundant fragment
   - **Feedback:** The Base peak is often the same as the M+ peak but not always!

8. **Question:** In Mass Spec, what does the M+ peak tell us?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     2. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     3. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     4. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     5. Mass of the molecule
   - **Correct Answer:** Mass of the molecule
   - **Feedback:** The M+ peak is the highest absorbance peak on the right side of the graph.

9. **Question:** In Mass Spec, what does the M+2 peak tell us?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     2. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     3. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     4. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     5. Presence of Cl or Br
   - **Correct Answer:** Presence of Cl or Br
   - **Feedback:** If M+ peak is 3x higher than M+2, Cl is present. If M+ peak is barely higher than M+2, Br is present.

10. **Question:** In UV-Vis, what does a high absorbance of high wavelengths indicate?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     2. More conjugation
     3. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     4. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     5. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
   - **Correct Answer:** More conjugation
   - **Feedback:** UV Vis: More conjugation = Larger wavelengths absorbed = lower energy absorbed

11. **Question:** In UV-Vis, what does a high absorbance of low wavelengths indicate?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     2. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     3. Less conjugation
     4. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     5. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
   - **Correct Answer:** Less conjugation
   - **Feedback:** UV Vis: Less conjugation = Smaller wavelengths absorbed = higher energy absorbed

12. **Question:** What colors, respectively, do acids and bases turn litmus paper?
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     2. Red and Blue
     3. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     4. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     5. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
   - **Correct Answer:** Red and Blue
   - **Feedback:** None / empty

13. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Anion-Exchange Chromatography
   - **Choices:**
     1. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     2. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     3. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     4. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     5. Most anionic elutes last, least anionic/most cationic elutes first.
   - **Correct Answer:** Most anionic elutes last, least anionic/most cationic elutes first.
   - **Feedback:** The name of the exchange is what's going held back in the column (eluting last), if it helps memorize the results.

14. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Cation-Exchange Chromatography
   - **Choices:**
     1. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     2. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     3. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     4. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     5. Most cationic elutes last, least cationic/most anionic elutes first.
   - **Correct Answer:** Most cationic elutes last, least cationic/most anionic elutes first.
   - **Feedback:** The name of the exchange is what's going held back in the column (eluting last), if it helps memorize the results.

15. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Flash Chromatography
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
     2. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     3. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     4. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     5. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
   - **Correct Answer:** Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
   - **Feedback:** Since the mobile phase in nearly all polarity chromatographies (except Reverse-Phase) is nonpolar and stationary phase is polar (MN vs SP), nonpolar will always elute first, polar last.

16. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Fractional Distillation
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     2. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     3. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     4. Molecules with the lowest boiling point and lowest number of intermolecular interactions elutes first.
     5. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
   - **Correct Answer:** Molecules with the lowest boiling point and lowest number of intermolecular interactions elutes first.
   - **Feedback:** Fractional is used for molecules with similar boiling points.

17. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Gas Chromatography
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
     2. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     3. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     4. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     5. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
   - **Correct Answer:** Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
   - **Feedback:** Since the mobile phase in nearly all polarity chromatographies (except Reverse-Phase) is nonpolar and stationary phase is polar (MN vs SP), nonpolar will always elute first, polar last.

18. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Liquid Chromatography
   - **Choices:**
     1. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     2. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     3. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     4. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     5. Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
   - **Correct Answer:** Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
   - **Feedback:** Since the mobile phase in nearly all polarity chromatographies (except Reverse-Phase) is nonpolar and stationary phase is polar (MN vs SP), nonpolar will always elute first, polar last.

19. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Normal-Phase HPLC
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     2. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     3. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     4. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     5. Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
   - **Correct Answer:** Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
   - **Feedback:** Since the mobile phase in nearly all polarity chromatographies (except Reverse-Phase) is nonpolar and stationary phase is polar (MN vs SP), nonpolar will always elute first, polar last.

20. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Reverse-Phase HPLC
   - **Choices:**
     1. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     2. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last.
     3. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     4. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     5. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
   - **Correct Answer:** Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last.
   - **Feedback:** The only polarity chromatography that has the most polar eluting first and nonpolar eluting last. All other polarity chromatographies have most nonpolar eluting first and most polar eluting last.

21. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Simple Distillation
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     2. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     3. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     4. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     5. Molecules with the lowest boiling point and lowest number of intermolecular interactions elutes first.
   - **Correct Answer:** Molecules with the lowest boiling point and lowest number of intermolecular interactions elutes first.
   - **Feedback:** Simple is used for molecules with very different boiling points than each other (difference is greater than 25C).

22. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Size-Exclusion Chromatography
   - **Choices:**
     1. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     2. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     3. Elution order is random and depends only on temperature
     4. Largest molecules elute first, smallest elute last.
     5. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
   - **Correct Answer:** Largest molecules elute first, smallest elute last.
   - **Feedback:** Aka Gel Filration Chromatography, large molecules can travel around the beads, smaller have to travel through them and will take longer.

23. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Thin-Layer Chromatography
   - **Choices:**
     1. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     2. Most cationic elutes first, least cationic elutes last.
     3. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     4. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     5. Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
   - **Correct Answer:** Most nonpolar elutes first, most polar elutes last.
   - **Feedback:** Since the mobile phase in nearly all polarity chromatographies (except Reverse-Phase) is nonpolar and stationary phase is polar (MN vs SP), nonpolar will always elute first, polar last.

24. **Question:** Which of the following correctly identifies the results of Vacuum Distillation
   - **Choices:**
     1. No separation occurs because both phases are identical
     2. Compounds elute strictly by molecular weight
     3. Most polar elutes first, most nonpolar elutes last
     4. Most polar elutes last, most nonpolar elutes first
     5. Molecules with the lowest boiling point and lowest number of intermolecular interactions elutes first.
   - **Correct Answer:** Molecules with the lowest boiling point and lowest number of intermolecular interactions elutes first.
   - **Feedback:** Vacuum is used whenever the molecules in question have very high boiling points that might cause them to decompose.
